IlluminaraI won't tell you it's talking. I haven't seen enough to say so, and you would be right not to believe me if I did. I'd like your dock-clock traffic log, Dr. Brannock's flare record, the specifications of every detector, and the room until the evening session.

Teodora VanceThe council sits at eighteen hundred. At eighteen hundred I put the evacuation to a vote.

IlluminaraThen I'll bring you something you can test before you vote.

Vance looked at the clock and then at her. Curiosity did not soften her face; it made the responsibility in it more exact.

Teodora VanceTest it with what? We have sixty-nine hours and fifty-one minutes.

02 Â· Nine Years of Margins

Ring observatory, Brannock's office Â· Day 1, 09:30

Ilse Brannock kept the flare record in a ledger as well as on the network, because the ledger could not be edited by anyone who disliked it. Nine years of the Wick filled it, night by night, in a small upright hand with questions in the margins. A few were underlined twice.

Ilse BrannockYou're the ice woman with the thermal models.

IlluminaraToday I'm the ice woman who read your flare record. It's very clean. Who keeps it?

Ilse BrannockI do. Nobody else wanted to. A flare is a flare.

IlluminaraYou don't think so.

Ilse put a hand flat on the ledger as one might on a sleeping animal.

Ilse BrannockI showed this to two people. One laughed, kindly, which was worse. I said the Wick grows louder when we do. After every busy evening the next burst is larger by about the same fraction. A star's flares don't care about our trade. I knew how it sounded, so I stopped saying it.

IlluminaraAnd the timing?

Ilse BrannockAlways in a gap. I called it the small hours because that was the gap we kept giving it.

Illuminara laid the traffic log over the flare record on the glass table. The observatory clock and dock clock were three minutes apart. Nobody had held the records together because the exchange net carried private trade.

She slid one trace by three minutes. The bright marks dropped into the troughs of the traffic like coins into a purse.

Ilse BrannockFour-oh-five. Shift change. The ring goes quiet for twenty minutes while the crews swap.

IlluminaraThe size follows the loudness before the gap. The timing follows the gap itself. If there is an intention here, the least extravagant account is not anger. It is turn-taking: something raising its voice over a crowd, then waiting not to be overwritten. But the same trace could still come from a rotating spot, a precursor that suppresses our band, or chance.

Ilse BrannockSay what you want to do.

IlluminaraMove the gap. Fill four-oh-five with ordinary traffic, open a new gap at seven, and write every prediction before we touch the switches.

Ilse BrannockThe exchange monitor shares the band. A precursor could fool it.

IlluminaraWhat does not share it?

Ilse BrannockThe old bolometric plate. Hard wire, separate clock, nothing in the exchange band. Rourke wanted it scrapped.

IlluminaraThen it stays. And you present the test. I will not borrow nine years of your listening and call it my discovery.

Ilse looked at the double-underlined question in her ledger. Hope is not evidence, Illuminara thought. But shame can hide evidence for years if nobody makes room for the person carrying it.

Ilse BrannockI will present it if the envelope contains the ways I may be wrong.

IlluminaraPut them first.

03 Â· The Case for Flight

Council chamber Â· Day 1, 18:00

Vance opened the session by reading the clock aloud: fifty-nine hours, ten minutes. Then she gave the floor to Rourke, who stood with his hands behind him, like a man giving evidence in a trial he hoped to lose.

Pell RourkeThe dangerous dawn alignment on Day Four, ordinary traffic, one point three of rating. Our two requested test gaps may draw smaller responses before it. If they do, that is new behavior, not the seventh and eighth members of the old sequence. It may strengthen the case for turn-taking, or mean the experiment itself is increasing the hazard.

Teodora VanceThat's the case for flight, and I will make it myself so nobody thinks I haven't. Evacuations fail when they start late. A star that changes because we ask it to is not automatically safer than a star that does not.

Gideon Farrow rose from the guild benches. He was round and beautifully dressed and had been counting something in his head for the whole session.

Gideon FarrowWho pays if we flee from a weather event? The ice is my guild's wages and the ring's drinking water. It can't be that nobody may ask whether the star is just a star.

Teodora VanceNobody has forbidden it. Dr. Brannock?

Ilse stood. Her hands were steady on the lectern, and Illuminara, three rows back, saw her find the notes she had meant not to need.

Ilse BrannockI have nine years of flares, three instruments and three clocks. The timing sits in the quiet of our traffic, and the size follows the loudness before it. I have three rivals: a spot rotating every twenty-four-point-one hours, a lull caused by a precursor rather than chosen by us, and chance.

Teodora VanceHow much chance?

Ilse BrannockI preserved the clustering in the nine-year record and shuffled ten thousand pairs of gaps with the same widths and traffic levels. Seven pairs caught bursts in both timing windows and both amplitude bands. Seven in ten thousand is not zero. It is also not an argument I would throw away.

Dov AchterbergA calculation built from the same ledger that suggested the question. And your windows are fifty minutes wide.

Ilse BrannockYes. Which is why the spot and precursor predictions are sealed beside ours. If the spot fires at the old hour, we lose. If the exchange band fails while the independent plate stays quiet, we lose. If one requested result misses, we write miss before we explain anything.

IlluminaraAt four-oh-five tomorrow we fill the usual gap with ordinary traffic. At seven we pause the exchange net for twenty-two minutes. The independent plate remains live. We repeat with a shorter gap after the next dusk peak. The council writes the stop conditions.

Gideon FarrowWhat does the pause cost my traders?

IlluminaraTell the room what they need to keep.

Gideon FarrowThe settlement hour. Move around it and I will sell the test to the guild. I won't sell a permanent blackout.

Dov AchterbergNor should he. A scheduled silence is a scheduled vulnerability. Medical calls, collision alarms, smugglers who know when nobody is looking. If the proposed cure requires fourteen thousand people to become deaf on a timetable, I prefer the ferries.

Illuminara turned toward him rather than back to the lectern.

IlluminaraThen the test must preserve an emergency carrier outside the crowded band, and any lasting arrangement must be movable. Nadim, can that be done without contaminating the plate?

Nadim OyelaranA narrow red channel, hard-limited to alarms and medical speech. The plate won't see it. For a lasting window, the Chair could choose within a ninety-minute band and announce it fifteen minutes ahead.

Teodora VanceNoted. My cancel line: any requested burst over nine-tenths, any burst outside both registered windows, any failure of the independent plate, or any failure of the red channel. The ferries are fuelled tonight and manifests cut. A trigger starts loading within one hour; completing it still takes twenty-six. Nobody edits that sentence afterward.

04 Â· The Gap Moves

Communications office, exchange net Â· Day 2, 06:52

At four-oh-five the old gap had been filled, and the Wick had said nothing. The exchange band stayed clean; so did the independent bolometric plate. Ilse had been awake since the previous morning and sat very straight in a chair she had not chosen.

Nadim OyelaranSealed envelope is in the council log. Rotation said four-oh-five, which did not happen. Precursor said the exchange band would fail there while the plate remained quiet, which did not happen. Turn-taking says seven, within twenty-five minutes, between half and seven-tenths of rating.

At seven the exchange net went quiet. Kitchens went on, ferries stayed fuelled, and the red emergency carrier whispered with two medical calls and one collision alarm. The trading floor stopped for twenty-two minutes as fourteen thousand people agreed to leave a space.

The hard-wired plate drew a flat line, then a flat line, and then a sharp stroke at seven-oh-two. The network monitor followed a fraction later. The long shoulder on the decay was the mark Ilse had drawn in her ledger for nine years.

Ilse BrannockFifty-eight hundredths on the plate.

Nadim OyelaranInside timing and amplitude. Red channel never dropped.

Nobody cheered. Illuminara held Ilse's elbow only after the older woman reached for her.

Ilse BrannockOne.

IlluminaraOne.

The second requested gap came at twenty-two thirty on the same day, after a smaller dusk peak. A weak rise appeared on the exchange monitor eleven minutes before the pause. Dov, watching from the council feed, called it a precursor. The hard-wired plate stayed level.

At twenty-two thirty-six the Wick flared. The registered amplitude band ended at forty hundredths. The plate climbed to forty-one.

Dov AchterbergOutside the envelope.

Ilse BrannockBy one hundredth. It is a miss.

Nadim OyelaranThe early rise was our own carrier leaking into the exchange monitor. It is absent from the plate. That defeats the precursor claim, but it does not put the amplitude back inside the envelope.

Pell RourkeAnd two requested responses in sixteen hours are faster than the old forty-four-hour background. If we are causing them, that matters more than whether we have taught the sun manners.

Illuminara studied the two traces and did not rescue her prediction.

IlluminaraRecord the timing hit, the amplitude miss, the failed precursor rival and the faster cadence. Then stop opening gaps until the council decides what the faster cadence means.

Ilse BrannockYou wanted a second night.

IlluminaraI wanted an answer that could tell us no. This one has.

05 Â· Forty Hours

Council chamber Â· Day 2, 23:10

Clock hour forty found the chamber full. On the wall were four lines, none softened: OLD-GAP SILENCE; FIRST REQUEST HIT; SECOND REQUEST TIMING HIT / AMPLITUDE MISS; REQUESTED CADENCE FASTER THAN BACKGROUND.

Teodora VanceNobody in this room gets to call that two clean predictions. Dr. Brannock, what survives?

Ilse BrannockThe rotating-spot prediction failed at four-oh-five. The precursor prediction failed on the independent plate. In ten thousand paired shuffles, seven matched both timing and amplitude; ours matched both timings but only one amplitude. The data still prefers schedule-response. It does not prove a mind, and the faster cadence may be a cost of asking.

Dov AchterbergThen stop asking and load the ferries. The experiment changed the thing we feared.

Pell RourkeOr exposed that it was already waiting for gaps we happened to give it. The Day Four burst remains the dangerous one. With the dusk cap and the parasol turned before dawn, my median is eight-tenths. Without them, one point three.

Dov AchterbergMedian. Your upper edge?

Pell RourkeNine-tenths. At the cancel line.

The two numbers stayed between them. Vance looked not at Illuminara but at Ilse.

Teodora VanceIf we stay, what do you ask the Wick to do?

Ilse BrannockNothing beyond what the dangerous burst may do anyway. One bounded silence at dawn, three pulses from our cutter, then no second request. If the flare comes in the window and under the line, we learn whether leaving room changes the load. If not, the manifests are already cut.

Gideon FarrowThe dusk cap costs the guild its best settlement of the quarter. We will take it once. Not daily, and not without the loss entered beside the science.

Dov AchterbergAnd no fixed daily blackout. The red channel stays open. Any later window moves inside the ninety-minute band, fifteen minutes' notice, renewed by vote each day. Security may cancel it without your permission.

Nadim OyelaranI can enforce that in hardware. Trade carrier dark; red carrier live; plate independent. Six signatures to change it.

IlluminaraThat is better than what I brought you.

Teodora VanceIt is safer because the people paying for it changed it.

Vance kept one hand on the written cancel line.

Teodora VanceThe ferries stay fuelled. Loading starts within one hour if the plate crosses nine-tenths, the flare leaves the window, the red carrier fails, or security calls the silence unsafe. Anyone may take a berth now. We attempt one bounded exchange at dawn. Dov's moving-window amendment and Farrow's cost ledger are part of the motion.

The vote passed seven to four, not nine to two. Dov voted no. His amendment passed eleven to zero.

Afterward the reply was designed in the communications office. Nadim had the forty-metre ice-cutting array. Ilse had her ledger open to the rising edge of the first requested flare.

Nadim OyelaranI can give it a beam. I can't tell you it will see it.

Ilse BrannockThree pulses on the rhythm of its own rise. Then silence for as long as the pulses took. Heard, and your turn.

IlluminaraNo claim about what it means.

Ilse BrannockNo. Only a courtesy we can withdraw.

06 Â· A Turn at Four-Oh-Five

Calloway Reach, communications office and Rakel Quay Â· Day 4, 04:05

Under ordinary traffic the dangerous burst had been forecast at one point three of parasol rating. Farrow's dusk cap cost the guild its best evening of the quarter. Turning the parasol cost a month's thruster propellant and left Rakel Quay outside its full shadow. Rourke's registered range was seven-tenths to nine-tenths. Nine-tenths remained the line.

The Chair chose four-oh-five from the ninety-minute band and announced it fifteen minutes before. The red carrier stayed live. In the communications office Nadim held the beam trigger. On Rakel Quay his sister Kemi and two riggers waited beside the relay-mast breakers, because the model said the exposed masts would take the first side-load.

Kemi OyelaranRakel ready. You owe me two masts if your polite sun sneezes.

Nadim OyelaranPut them on Farrow's ledger.

Gideon FarrowThey are already there.

At four-oh-five Nadim sent three pulses. The trade band remained silent afterward. A medical call crossed the red carrier from nursery six; a child had swallowed a button. The silence did not make the ring helpless.

Four minutes passed.

The independent plate climbed.

Pell RourkePoint six. Point seven-two. Parasol taking thirty-one percent.

A second shoulder rose under the first.

Teodora VanceRead the plate, not the model.

Pell RourkePoint eight-six.

At Rakel, the first mast began to arc. Kemi cut its breaker before the automatic relay understood what was happening. The mast folded across an empty loading rail. The second leaned toward a fuel line.

Kemi OyelaranLosing west two. Clearing the line now.

She drove the quay tug into the mast's lower brace. Metal went down away from the fuel line, taking the tug's manipulator arm with it.

Nadim OyelaranKemi?

Kemi OyelaranStill expensive.

The plate touched eighty-nine hundredths. For twelve seconds its uncertainty band crossed the line, though the central reading did not.

Teodora VanceRourke.

Pell RourkeCentral reading below nine. Structure stable. My recommendation is hold, Chair. Your line says over, not near.

Vance's finger rested on the loading order. She could have called uncertainty a trigger. Nobody would have blamed her.

Teodora VanceHold. Kemi has the quay. Red carrier is live. If the central plate rises one hundredth, load.

It did not. The long shoulder fell through eight-tenths, then seven. The parasol held. Rakel lost two relay masts and one tug arm; no one was hurt.

In the decay, with Nadim's array still silent and the ring keeping the space it had promised, the independent plate drew three small steps.

Ilse BrannockThree. I have nine years and it has never made three.

IlluminaraDon't read it yet. We don't know that it's a reply.

Ilse BrannockNo. We know only that it happened after our three.

They did not repeat the request that night. The next day the council authorized one movable window, with the red channel and security veto intact, and required a fresh vote every seven days. Farrow entered the lost settlement, two masts, a tug arm and a month of propellant in the same ledger as the avoided evacuation. Dov signed the safety protocol and wrote beneath his name that he still believed they should leave.

Teodora VanceHe is allowed to be wrong in the record, if he is wrong.

IlluminaraSo are we.

Ilse closed her ledger, opened it again, and wrote on the next clean line in her small upright hand: Four-oh-five. We stopped. It knocked.

T05 Â· AUTHOR_FINAL

Distinct authentic author text preserved from the source event.

THE STAR THAT ONLY SPOKE IN FLARES

A ring habitat of fourteen thousand has seventy hours to decide whether its star is attacking it, and the only evidence that it is not is a silence nobody had thought to measure.

01 Â· Seventy Hours

Calloway Reach, Ring Council chamber Â· Day 1, 06:10

The clock on the chamber wall read 70:00:00. Someone had put it there so that it would be read aloud, and nobody had. Outside the long window the ring turned slowly under a red sun the size of a coin, with fourteen thousand people on its inner face, and the room smelled of cold tea and of a council that had not slept.

Illuminara had come aboard three weeks earlier on an ice tender, hired by the Rakel cooperative to model how heat and light loaded the cutting quays. She was in the chamber because her models were the only ones nobody had yet argued with. She stood at the back, beside the water jug.

Teodora VanceThe Wick has thrown six dangerous bursts in eleven days. In nine years of records it threw about one a month. Eleven days is two hundred and sixty-four hours; six bursts in that time is one every forty-four, and that is not a fluctuation I can fold into the background. They cluster after our evening traffic peaks. The next natural alignment is due at about four in the morning on Day Four. Under ordinary traffic the shield office forecasts one point three times the rating of our parasol. Ferries need twenty-six hours to finish loading, and I will not cut a two-hour margin out of a ring's lives. The order has to go out by hour forty-two.

Pell RourkeThe parasol takes one burst. I will not promise it takes that one.

IlluminaraWhat does leaving cost?

Teodora VanceRakel. Everything we mine. A ring that leaves carries eight months of water, and after that it's a very large cup.

Vance was sixty and square-shouldered, with the habit of putting the worst number first so that no one could accuse her of hiding it. She turned from the window to face the back of the room.

Teodora VanceDon't tell me it's talking. We have no way to know it is speaking at all, and you are asking fourteen thousand people to stay under a possible weapon on the strength of a pattern. If I'm wrong about the weapon we lose the ice and a year. If you're wrong we lose the ring.

IlluminaraI won't tell you it's talking. I haven't seen enough to say so, and you would be right not to believe me if I did. I'd like your dock-clock traffic log, Dr. Brannock's flare record, the specifications of every detector, and the room until the evening session.

Teodora VanceThe council sits at eighteen hundred. At eighteen hundred I put the evacuation to a vote.

IlluminaraThen I'll bring you something you can test before you vote.

Vance looked at the clock and then at her. Curiosity did not soften her face; it made the responsibility in it more exact.

Teodora VanceTest it with what? We have sixty-nine hours and fifty-one minutes.

02 Â· Nine Years of Margins

Ring observatory, Brannock's office Â· Day 1, 09:30

Ilse Brannock kept the flare record in a ledger as well as on the network, because the ledger could not be edited by anyone who disliked it. Nine years of the Wick filled it, night by night, in a small upright hand with questions in the margins. A few were underlined twice.

Ilse BrannockYou're the ice woman with the thermal models.

IlluminaraToday I'm the ice woman who read your flare record. It's very clean. Who keeps it?

Ilse BrannockI do. Nobody else wanted to. A flare is a flare.

IlluminaraYou don't think so.

Ilse put a hand flat on the ledger as one might on a sleeping animal.

Ilse BrannockI showed this to two people. One laughed, kindly, which was worse. I said the Wick grows louder when we do. After every busy evening the next burst is larger by about the same fraction. A star's flares don't care about our trade. I knew how it sounded, so I stopped saying it.

IlluminaraAnd the timing?

Ilse BrannockAlways in a gap. I called it the small hours because that was the gap we kept giving it.

Illuminara laid the traffic log over the flare record on the glass table. The observatory clock and dock clock were three minutes apart. Nobody had held the records together because the exchange net carried private trade.

She slid one trace by three minutes. The bright marks dropped into the troughs of the traffic like coins into a purse.

Ilse BrannockFour-oh-five. Shift change. The ring goes quiet for twenty minutes while the crews swap.

IlluminaraThe size follows the loudness before the gap. The timing follows the gap itself. If there is an intention here, the least extravagant account is not anger. It is turn-taking: something raising its voice over a crowd, then waiting not to be overwritten. But the same trace could still come from a rotating spot, a precursor that suppresses our band, or chance.

Ilse BrannockSay what you want to do.

IlluminaraMove the gap. Fill four-oh-five with ordinary traffic, open a new gap at seven, and write every prediction before we touch the switches.

Ilse BrannockThe exchange monitor shares the band. A precursor could fool it.

IlluminaraWhat does not share it?

Ilse BrannockThe old bolometric plate. Hard wire, separate clock, nothing in the exchange band. Rourke wanted it scrapped.

IlluminaraThen it stays. And you present the test. I will not borrow nine years of your listening and call it my discovery.

Ilse looked at the double-underlined question in her ledger. Hope is not evidence, Illuminara thought. But shame can hide evidence for years if nobody makes room for the person carrying it.

Illuminara turned the ledger's pages back to the worst flares and forward to the Day Four forecast, and stopped. The test would tell them whether the star kept time. It would not shrink the burst already coming.

IlluminaraOne more thing, and it is a question about your own margins. If the size follows the loudness before the gap, then the Day Four burst is not fixed. It is being sized right now by what we do on the evening of Day Three. What happens to the flare after a quiet evening?

Ilse turned eight pages without looking for them. The smallest flares in the book were all there: the nights after the ring had gone dark for the harvest festival, when the trade band was nearly empty by dusk.

Ilse BrannockSmall. They're the smallest in the book. I put them down to the calendar.

IlluminaraThen the lever is the dusk peak, not the gap. If the council can cap one evening's traffic before the Day Four alignment, the parasol may meet something near eight-tenths instead of one point three. Pell will have to model it. I can't promise it. I can tell you which number to try to move.

Ilse BrannockI will present it if the envelope contains the ways I may be wrong.

IlluminaraPut them first.

03 Â· The Case for Flight

Council chamber Â· Day 1, 18:00

Vance opened the session by reading the clock aloud: fifty-eight hours, ten minutes. Then she gave the floor to Rourke, who stood with his hands behind him, like a man giving evidence in a trial he hoped to lose.

Pell RourkeThe dangerous dawn alignment on Day Four, ordinary traffic, one point three of rating. Our two requested test gaps may draw smaller responses before it. If they do, that is new behavior, not the seventh and eighth members of the old sequence. It may strengthen the case for turn-taking, or mean the experiment itself is increasing the hazard.

Teodora VanceThat's the case for flight, and I will make it myself so nobody thinks I haven't. Evacuations fail when they start late. A star that changes because we ask it to is not automatically safer than a star that does not.

Gideon Farrow rose from the guild benches. He was round and beautifully dressed and had been counting something in his head for the whole session.

Gideon FarrowWho pays if we flee from a weather event? The ice is my guild's wages and the ring's drinking water. It can't be that nobody may ask whether the star is just a star.

Teodora VanceNobody has forbidden it. Dr. Brannock?

Ilse stood. Her hands were steady on the lectern, and Illuminara, three rows back, saw her find the notes she had meant not to need.

Ilse BrannockI have nine years of flares, three instruments and three clocks. The timing sits in the quiet of our traffic, and the size follows the loudness before it. I have three rivals: a spot rotating every twenty-four-point-one hours, a lull caused by a precursor rather than chosen by us, and chance.

Teodora VanceHow much chance?

Ilse BrannockI kept the clustering and the one-a-month background of the nine-year record, and shuffled ten thousand pairs of gaps with the same widths and traffic levels. Seven pairs caught bursts in both timing windows and both amplitude bands. Seven in ten thousand is not zero. It is also not an argument I would throw away.

Dov AchterbergA calculation built from the same ledger that suggested the question. And your windows are fifty minutes wide.

Ilse BrannockYes. Which is why the spot and precursor predictions are sealed beside ours. If the spot fires at the old hour, we lose. If the exchange band fails while the independent plate stays quiet, we lose. If one requested result misses, we write miss before we explain anything.

IlluminaraAt four-oh-five tomorrow we fill the usual gap with ordinary traffic. At seven we pause the exchange net for twenty-two minutes. The independent plate remains live. We repeat with a shorter gap after the next dusk peak. And there is a second lever, which the ledger itself points to. The size follows the loudness before the gap, so the Day Four burst is being sized by the evening of Day Three. If the guild caps that one dusk peak, Pell's median should fall well below the parasol's rating. That is a prediction too, and it can fail. The council writes the stop conditions.

Gideon FarrowWhat does the pause cost my traders? And what does your cap cost them?

IlluminaraTell the room what they need to keep.

Gideon FarrowThe settlement hour. Move around it and I will sell the test to the guild. The cap I will price tonight, and the number will not be small: that dusk is my best evening of the quarter. I won't sell a permanent blackout, and I won't sell a permanent cap.

Dov AchterbergNor should he. A scheduled silence is a scheduled vulnerability. Medical calls, collision alarms, smugglers who know when nobody is looking. If the proposed cure requires fourteen thousand people to become deaf on a timetable, I prefer the ferries.

Illuminara turned toward him rather than back to the lectern.

IlluminaraThen the test must preserve an emergency carrier outside the crowded band, and any lasting arrangement must be movable. Nadim, can that be done without contaminating the plate?

Nadim OyelaranA narrow red channel, hard-limited to alarms and medical speech. The plate won't see it. For a lasting window, the Chair could choose within a ninety-minute band and announce it fifteen minutes ahead.

Teodora VanceNoted. My cancel line: any requested burst over nine-tenths, any burst outside both registered windows, any failure of the independent plate, or any failure of the red channel. The ferries are fuelled tonight and manifests cut. A trigger starts loading within one hour; completing it still takes twenty-six. Nobody edits that sentence afterward.

04 Â· The Gap Moves

Communications office, exchange net Â· Day 2, 06:52

At four-oh-five the old gap had been filled, and the Wick had said nothing. The exchange band stayed clean; so did the independent bolometric plate. Ilse had been awake since the previous morning and sat very straight in a chair she had not chosen.

Nadim OyelaranSealed envelope is in the council log. Rotation said four-oh-five, which did not happen. Precursor said the exchange band would fail there while the plate remained quiet, which did not happen. Turn-taking says seven, within twenty-five minutes, between half and seven-tenths of rating.

The pause had been posted in the council log the evening before, so that every office would know to expect it. At seven the exchange net went quiet. Kitchens went on, ferries stayed fuelled, and the red emergency carrier whispered with two medical calls and one collision alarm. The trading floor stopped for twenty-two minutes as fourteen thousand people agreed to leave a space.

The hard-wired plate drew a flat line, then a flat line, and then a sharp stroke at seven-oh-two. The network monitor followed a fraction later. The long shoulder on the decay was the mark Ilse had drawn in her ledger for nine years.

Ilse BrannockFifty-eight hundredths on the plate.

Nadim OyelaranInside timing and amplitude. Red channel never dropped.

Nobody cheered. Illuminara held Ilse's elbow only after the older woman reached for her.

Ilse BrannockOne.

IlluminaraOne.

At seven-oh-six a cargo tug on the outer rail killed its transponder and began drifting toward the customs lock. Dov's security desk saw it only because the lock's alarm line rode the red carrier, and the desk had four minutes to seal the lock before the tug's crew reached it.

Dov AchterbergLock six sealed. Tug has gone back to its berth. They knew our hour because we posted our hour.

He said it to the room, not to Illuminara, and he did not say I told you. The tug's name went into the log beside the minutes.

The second requested gap came at twenty-two thirty on the same day, after a smaller dusk peak than the evening before: a stalled ferry queue the guild had not planned. A weak rise appeared on the exchange monitor eleven minutes before the pause. Dov, watching from the council feed, called it a precursor. The hard-wired plate stayed level.

At twenty-two thirty-six the Wick flared. The registered amplitude band ended at forty hundredths. The plate climbed to forty-one.

Dov AchterbergOutside the envelope.

Ilse BrannockBy one hundredth. It is a miss.

Nadim OyelaranThe early rise was our own carrier leaking into the exchange monitor. It is absent from the plate. That defeats the precursor claim, but it does not put the amplitude back inside the envelope.

Pell RourkeAnd two requested responses in sixteen hours are faster than even the forty-four-hour cluster we started with. If we are causing them, that matters more than whether we have taught the sun manners.

Illuminara studied the two traces and did not rescue her prediction. Beside the second trace Ilse had written the evening's peak, and beside the first, the evening before. She set the two numbers side by side and left them without comment.

Ilse BrannockFifty-eight hundredths after the loud evening. Forty-one after the quiet one.

Pell RourkeOne pair.

Ilse BrannockOne pair. It fell the right way. It is not a law, and I will not write it as one.

IlluminaraRecord the timing hit, the amplitude miss, the failed precursor rival, the faster cadence and the pair of evenings. Then stop opening gaps until the council decides what the faster cadence means.

Ilse BrannockYou wanted a second night.

IlluminaraI wanted an answer that could tell us no. This one has.

05 Â· Forty Hours

Council chamber Â· Day 2, 23:10

Clock hour forty-one found the chamber full, with twenty-nine hours left on the wall and one hour before the order would have to go out. Under the clock were four lines, none softened: OLD-GAP SILENCE; FIRST REQUEST HIT; SECOND REQUEST TIMING HIT / AMPLITUDE MISS; REQUESTED CADENCE FASTER THAN BACKGROUND.

Teodora VanceNobody in this room gets to call that two clean predictions. Dr. Brannock, what survives?

Ilse BrannockThe rotating-spot prediction failed at four-oh-five. The precursor prediction failed on the independent plate. In ten thousand paired shuffles, seven matched both timing and amplitude; ours matched both timings but only one amplitude. The data still prefers schedule-response. It does not prove a mind, and the faster cadence may be a cost of asking.

Dov AchterbergThen stop asking and load the ferries. The experiment changed the thing we feared. And it told every smuggler on the outer rail what hour to move.

Pell RourkeOr exposed that it was already waiting for gaps we happened to give it. The Day Four burst remains the dangerous one. Capping the Day Three dusk peak alone takes my median from one point three to about one point zero. Turning the parasol before dawn takes it to about nine-tenths. What the bounded silence adds I cannot separate from the rest, and I will not pretend to. Together, eight-tenths.

Dov AchterbergMedian. Your upper edge?

Pell RourkeNine-tenths. At the cancel line.

The two numbers stayed between them. Vance looked not at Illuminara but at Ilse.

Teodora VanceIf we stay, what do you ask the Wick to do?

Ilse BrannockNothing beyond what the dangerous burst may do anyway. One bounded silence at dawn, three pulses from our cutter, then no second request.
